7～9 月期実質GDP は年率＋1.0％と 
プラス成長を予想（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比＋0.2％（年率＋1.0％）と、4～6月期（前期比＋
0.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半期連続でのプラス成長を予想 
○ 個人消費が伸び悩む一方、設備投資や民間在庫が押し上げる見込み。なお、公的在庫と輸入では
中東情勢を主因とする一時的な変動が生じ、成長率にプラス・マイナス両面で影響 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）はプラス成長を
予想 
11月16日（月）に、内閣府から2026年7～9月期の「四半期別GDP速 報（ QE）」
（1次速報値）が公表される。現時点で入手可能な基礎統計の結果を踏まえ
ると、7～9月期の実質GDPは前期比＋0.2％（ 年 率 ＋1.0％）となる見込みだ。
4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半
期連続でプラス成長になると予想する（図表）。 
7～9月期は、内需の柱である個人消費が伸び悩む一方、設備投資の拡大や、
製造業を中心とする在庫積み増しが成長率を押し上げるとみられる。その他
では、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要因として現れる
が、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイナス要因になるなど、

前期比に対する寄与度（％Pt）

### Page 2

2

中東情勢による一時的な変動も引き続き成長率に影響する見込みだ。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。 
個人消費は伸び悩み。耐
久財の特需剥落や、根強
い節約志向が影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％（ 4～6月期：同 ＋0.0％）と、2四半期連続で伸び
悩む結果になろう。消費の弱さの背景には、複合的な要因があると考えられ
る。 
耐久財では、自動車とエアコンの「特需」剥落が押し下げ要因になろう。
自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に廃止されたこと
を受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていたが、8月以降は再
び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年4月の省エネ基準
引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要が生じていたが、
景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入った後は一服した
模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減少もエアコンなど
季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
食品やエネルギー、各種消耗品などの非耐久財では、物価高を背景とする
根強い節約志向により、弱含みが続いているようだ。今回のインフレ局面が
本格化する前の2020年と比較すると、非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6
年半でおよそ1.3倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上
昇が、消費者の体感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、購入数量の抑制
につながっている可能性がある。 
サービス消費は、イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、サービスや価格帯、顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
設備投資は前期比＋1.2％（ 4～6月 期：同 ▲0.9％）と、3四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスへ転じると予想する。 
直近の設備投資関連指標をみると、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財
総供給指数（除く輸送機械）が7～8月に大きく上昇した。また第3次産業活
動指数では、ソフトウェア業の実質売上高が同じく7～8月に増加したことが
確認でき、どちらも7～9月期の設備投資が拡大したことを示唆している。 
加えて、前期の4～6月期に発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特許権の海外
譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少）がなくなったこともプラス要因だ。た
だし、これは裏を返せば、7～9月期のQEにおける設備投資の伸びが実態以上
に強い数字になるということでもあり、評価する際には留意が必要である。 
住宅投資は前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.8％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.6％ ）と 、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスとなろう。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～8月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上

### Page 3

3

昇による需要低迷に加えて、足元では地震や台風などによる着工・進捗の遅
れが発生している可能性がある。 
民間在庫は2四半期連続
で成長率にプラス寄与 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、＋0.2％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラスが続く見込みだ。AI関連需要の強さなどを背景に、製
造業で在庫積み増しの動きが進んでいることが影響すると考えられる。な
お、4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を
押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足
が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響は一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラスに 
公的需要は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.8％）と、2四半期ぶりの前期
比プラスを予想する。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じるとみられることだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変
動の寄与度が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備
蓄の減少が小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公
的在庫の減少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.3％Pt程度のプラ
ス寄与になると見込まれる。 
その他の公的需要項目では、政府消費を前期比＋0.2％（4～6月期：同＋
1.7％）と予想する。4～6月期は高校授業料・小学校給食費の無償化開始に
より高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした特殊要因が剥落し、緩やかな
増加ペースに戻ると考えられる。公共投資は前期比▲0.5％（4～6月期：同
▲0.5％）と、土木関連工事の減少を受けて2四半期連続のマイナスを見込む。 
輸出が小幅に減少する一
方、輸入が増加し、外需は
4四半期ぶりのマイナス
寄与 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.3％（4～6月期：同＋0.4％）と小幅に減少す
る一方、輸 入 が同＋1.3％（ 4～6月 期：同▲1.7％）と 増加に転じる見込みだ。
実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.3％Ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナ
スが予想される。 
輸入の増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化により4
～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホルム
ズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの輸入
量が確保されている。 
輸入はGDPの控除項目であることから、7～9月期は輸入増加が成長率の下
押し要因になる。ただし、代替調達の進展に伴って国家備蓄原油の放出が抑
制され、先述したように7～9月期は公的在庫変動が成長率にプラス寄与する
とみられることから、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果の一部は公的在庫変
動により相殺されるだろう。 
輸出では、前期の4～6月期に特許権の海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げ
た（これは4～6月期の設備投資の押し下げ要因でもある）反動が現れ、一時
的に下振れするだろう。一方、旺盛なAI関連需要が支えになり、半導体・電

### Page 4

4

子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸出は堅調に推移する見込みだ。インバウ
ンド需要（非居住者家計の国内での直接購入）は、日中対立の継続による中
国人訪日客の少なさに加え、航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の伸び悩みに
より、低調な結果になると見込まれる。 
2026 年度後半の実質GDP
は小幅プラスと予想。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期から減速するものの、4四半期
連続でプラス成長になると予想する。GDPの約半分を占める個人消費の伸び
悩みが足を引っ張っているが、設備投資や製造業を中心とする在庫積み増し
がけん引する格好だ。 
先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～2027年3月）の 実質 GDP
は、均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。個人消費が振るわな
い一方で、設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろう。2026年度全体の
実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平均水準が押し上げら
れることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％程度とまずまずの
成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。原油・ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、コア消費者物価指数（生鮮食品を除く総合）は年度末にかけて前年比＋
3％程度まで上昇すると予想される（ただし、2026年末から政府の電気・ガ
ス料金支援策が実施されれば、0.4～0.5％Pt程度の押し下げ要因になる可能
性がある）。加えて、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを
中心とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、一時的に消費が落ち込む可能
性がある。 
他方で、年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調
査）では、2026年度の設備投資計画（ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投
資額（除く土地投資額））が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した（6月 調 査：
同＋8.8％）。生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、
設備の不足感が強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、設備投資は今後拡大
傾向で推移していく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」（特定生産性向上
設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、また、投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。

### Page 5

5

日銀は10月会合で政策金
利を据え置き。次回利上
げは12月と予想 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％）は、次回12
月17・18日の会合と予想する。前回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて「3カ月
に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の「半年強
に1回」から加速していることを印象付けるものになろう。 
企業の価格転嫁行動は一段と積極化しており、植田総裁が9月会合後の記
者会見で「政策の局面が変化した」と説明したように、日本銀行は「物価引
き上げ」から「物価上振れ抑制」へと金融政策運営の舵を切っている。12月
の追加利上げ後も、金利上昇の影響を確認しながら、日銀は最終的に2027年
半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％まで引き上げていくだろう。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

Document 065: 2026年10月贸易数据点评：外贸增长的价格权重持续上升

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月贸易数据点评：外贸增长的价格权重持续上升.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
ooo[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 11 月 10 日      
证 券研究报告•宏观简评报告     
数据点评 
外贸增长的价格权重持续上升 
——2026 年 10 月贸易数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院 
[Table_Summary]  摘要：从贸易总体情况看，以美元计，2026 年 1—10 月我国货物贸易进出口
总额同比增长 25.3%，较 1—9 月扩大 1.5 个百分点。10 月单月出口同比增长
35.2%，进口同比增长 41.6%，分别较上月回落 1.1 个百分点、加快 10.6 个百
分点，进口增速创 2021 年 6 月以来新高，贸易顺差收窄至 1052.8 亿美元。展
望后续，去年同期低基数及主要经济体制造业 PMI 仍处扩张区间，有望支撑
11 月外贸增速维持较高水平，但前期集中出货效应减弱、海外需求波动及贸易
政策不确定性或带来扰动。从贸易伙伴看，10 月我国对主要贸易伙伴出口增
速普遍回落，但对韩国出口同比增长 92.4%，较上月加快 24.3 个百分点；自
韩国进口同比增长 110.2%，较上月加快 15.9 个百分点。对韩贸易高增主要受
AI 产业链涨价及加工贸易重复计算效应共同推动，若后续 AI 硬件价格见顶回
落，对韩名义贸易增速或随之放缓。从出口结构看，机电产品、高新技术产品
出口金额同比分别增长 46.1%、66.6%，自动数据处理设备及其零部件、集成
电路出口分别增长 114.8%、137.1%，AI 算力基础设施建设持续提供需求支撑。
不过，集成电路出口数量同比下降 2.5%，金额高增与实际出口数量增长存在
明显背离。汽车及零配件出口金额分别增长 37.6%、32.1%，通用机械设备出
口增长 34.5%，出口动能有所扩散；手机、家电等传统消费电子出口增速边际
放缓，劳动密集型产品出口表现相对偏弱。从进口结构看，机电产品、高新技
术产品进口金额同比涨幅分别扩大至 57.5%、72.1%，集成电路、自动数据处
理设备及其零部件进口分别增长 78.3%、261.0%，但集成电路进口数量同比
持平，反映 AI 产业链进口增长尚未同步体现为实物数量扩张。资源品方面，
原油、成品油进口金额分别增长 41.0%、45.2%，但进口数量仍分别下降 13.3%、
10.2%；铁矿石进口金额同比下降 11.5%，铜及铜材进口金额增长 45.6%，资
源品进口表现分化。汽车进口金额和数量分别下降 38.5%、43.1%，农产品及
粮食、大豆进口也有所收缩。整体来看，10 月外贸增长仍主要由 AI产业链及
部分资源品价格上涨支撑，出口结构向电子产业链集中，进口则呈现高技术产
品偏强、传统资源品及汽车偏弱的分化特征，剔除价格因素后，实际贸易需求
仍有待进一步改善。 
 进口超预期提速，顺差收窄。按美元计，2026 年 1-10 月，我国货物贸易进出
口总额同比增长 25.3%，涨幅相比 1‑ 9 月扩大 1.5 个百分点，持续保持高位。
10 月单月，货物贸易出口同比增长 35.2%，较上月回落 1.1 个百分点，高于
wind 一致预测值（33.25%）；进口同比增长 41.6%，较上月回升 10.6 个百分
点，显著高于 wind 一致预测值（30.36%），创 2021 年 6 月以来最快同比增
速。贸易顺差为 1052.8 亿美元，虽高于去年同期，低于 wind 一致预测值（1195
亿美元），为 5 月以来最小单月顺差。关税与贸易谈判的不确定性催生企业前
置出货，是今年出口高增的重要推手之一；不过随着窗口期临近，前期订单已
逐渐兑现，该支撑的边际贡献逐步回落。同时，近期人民币升值放缓对美元计
价出口的折算贡献有所回落。进口端的高增则主要来自 AI 产业链产品与资源
品涨价，叠加去年同期低基数的驱动。从海外国际景气表现来看，10 月美欧
日制造业景气均有所回落：标普全球美国制造业 PMI 下修至 54.9，新订单增
速降至三月以来最低水平，反映出关税对国际销售的影响。能源、燃料、运输

2
和金属价格上涨叠加关税影响，推动投入成本通胀升至五月以来最高水平；欧
元区制造业 PMI 终值回落至 52.7，产出增速放缓至四个月低点，但整体仍保
持稳健；日本制造业 PMI 终值回落至 53.3，企业产出和新订单增幅均放缓，
且增长速度都降至七个月来最低。贸易政策方面，11 月 9 日国务院关税税则
委员会发布公告，宣布自 11 月 10 日起 2 个月内继续暂停 24%的对美加征关
税、保留 10%税率。此次展期安排有助于稳定外贸企业对美订单与产能预期，
为后续谈判保留政策缓冲期。近期美方同步将 178 项对华 301 关税豁免延期至
2027 年 1 月 9 日，豁免清单以工业中间品、医疗用品、光伏制造设备为主，
有助于降低相关品类对美出口关税成本。往后看，去年四季度基数较低，主要
经济体制造业 PMI 也仍在扩张区间，11 月贸易增速仍有望维持在较高水平，
但动能或边际放缓。出口方面，存储芯片价格上涨仍将提供支撑。进口方面，
芯片需求有望继续支撑年底进口增速，而原油价格环比走弱可能拖累资源品进

口金额。整体来看，AI 硬件仍是影响进出口表现的核心变量，但剔除价格因
素后，真实外需与内需信号或仍相对偏弱。
 单月对韩贸易实现高增，低基数叠加 AI 链涨价共同支撑。以美元计，从出口
总额看，10 月单月我国对主要贸易伙伴出口同比增速普遍边际回落，但是对
韩国出口同比增速边际加快。对美国、东盟出口同比增速分别回落 6.6、5.9
个百分点至 32.9%、46.3%。对韩出口增速加快 24.3 个百分点至 92.4%。从
进口总额看，除欧盟以外，10 月单月我国对主要贸易伙伴的进口同比增速均
加快。对韩国、美国、日本、东盟进口同比增速分别加快 15.9、14.2、11.1、
5.8 个百分点至 110.2%、35.0%、26.5%、42.0%。从韩国前 20 日贸易数据看，
10 月 1-20 日，韩国半导体出口额同比增长 222.5%，占出口总额的 44.7%。
本月我国自韩国进口金额高增主要受 AI 产业链产品价格上涨推动，对韩出口
高增则来自加工贸易的重复计算效应。同一批芯片的中间形态在双边贸易统计

中可能被计入了不止一次，而芯片价格的上涨会同时放大进口额和出口额。若
后续 AI 硬件价格见顶回落，对韩名义出口增速可能随之回落，且实际贸易量
未必出现同等幅度的收缩。此外，10 月中欧就混动汽车贸易安排达成谅解，
拟设置首年 35万辆的进口配额，次年起配额每年上调 6%，措施最早拟于 12
月 1 日生效。该配额机制名义适用于所有来源地混动进口，因此对欧汽车出口
的边际拖累或在年底至明年初逐步显现。
 出口品类集中度提升，AI 产业链为景气主线。从出口金额看，以美元计，10
月单月机电产品、高新技术产品出口金额同比分别增长 46.1%、66.6%，增速
较上月分别加快 2.4、3.0 个百分点，占出口总额的比重分别为 67.42%、33.56%
（两类统计口径存在交叉，部分产品同时归入两类），说明出口高增品类进一
步向电子链条集中。分产品看，AI相关电子产品出口表现突出，自动数据处理
设备及其零部件、集成电路出口金额同比分别增长 114.8%、137.1%，增速较
上月分别加快 21.8、1.1 个百分点。传统消费电子出口增速则有所放缓，手机、

家用电器、音视频设备及其零件出口金额同比分别增长 13.2%、20.9%、12.1%，
增速较上月均边际放缓。汽车包括底盘、汽车零配件出口金额同比分别增长
37.6%、32.1%，而包括箱包、服装、鞋靴等品类的传统劳动密集型产品同比
增速均边际回落，显示传统轻工外需承压。通用机械设备出口金额同比上升
34.5%，增速边际加快 19 个百分点，或反映海外制造业资本开支回暖，全球
中游设备需求边际改善。成品油出口金额同比涨幅收窄 44.8 个百分点至
80.3%。从出口数量看，重点电子产品呈现明显量价分化。集成电路出口数量
同比由上月上升 0.9%转为下降 2.5%，说明本月集成电路出口金额的爆发几乎
完全由单价上涨贡献。手机出口数量同比下降 18.2%，降幅进一步扩大，出口
金额增长主要受高端机型占比提升和产品单价上涨带动，而出口数量降幅扩
大，则反映全球手机终端需求仍显疲弱，也可能与部分低端组装订单持续向海
外转移有关。汽车包括底盘、家用电器出口数量同比分别上升 33.5%、14.1%，

但涨幅均较上月收窄，或由于节前出货、赶关税窗口等因素释放后，增速自然
回落。其中，汽车品类出口量价齐升，但汽车出口数量增速中枢已从二季度的

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
极端水平回落，或反映汽车出口增量逻辑从单纯放量逐步向结构升级、单价抬
升切换。往后看，年内出口结构或延续向电子产业链集中的特征，但需关注价
格上涨与实际出口数量的背离。传统消费电子需求偏弱，手机出口数量或继续
承压；汽车出口则受前期集中出货效应消退影响，增速可能进一步回落。
 涨价驱动 AI 链进口高增，资源品进口读数受价格支撑。从进口金额看，以美
元计，机电产品、高新技术产品进口金额同比涨幅分别扩大至 57.5%、72.1%，
反映进口增长进一步向电子产业链集中。分产品看，AI相关电子产品进口保持
高增长，集成电路进口金额同比涨幅小幅扩大至 78.3%，自动数据处理设备及
其零部件进口金额同比上升 261.0%，涨幅较上月收窄 35.5 个百分点。能源资
源品进口金额有所回升，原油进口金额同比上升 41.0%，涨幅较上月扩大 37.7
个百分点，成品油进口金额同比由上月下降转为上升 45.2%。铁矿砂及其精矿
进口金额同比下降 11.5%，降幅较上月扩大，说明黑色系原料的进口价值量有

所收缩；铜矿砂及其精矿进口金额同比上升 23.1%，涨幅较上月收窄，未锻造
的铜及铜材进口金额同比上升 45.6%，涨幅较上月扩大。10 月 LME 铜期货结
算均价同比上升 35.9%，涨幅较上月收窄约 10 个百分点，但价格同比仍维持
较高正增，或指向铜进口金额高增主要由铜价处于历史高位拉动。汽车包括底
盘进口金额同比下降 38.5%，降幅较上月扩大；农产品、粮食和大豆进口金额
同比均由上月上升转为下降。从进口数量看，电子产品进口呈现价格强、数量
弱的特征。集成电路进口数量同比持平，说明 AI 资本开支周期对价格周期与
订单节奏高度敏感，而实物数量的需求并未同步显著扩张。能源品进口金额增
长主要受到价格推动。原油、成品油进口数量同比分别下降 13.3%、10.2%，
降幅较上月分别收窄 8.2、42.9 个百分点。10 月布伦特原油现货均价为 125.0
美元/桶，同比上升 92.3%，涨幅较上月扩大近 20 个百分点。在原油进口数量
同比仍在下降的背景下，原油进口金额高增主要由价格弹性主导。汽车及农产

品进口则延续数量收缩。汽车包括底盘进口数量同比下降 43.1%，降幅较上月
收窄 6.9 个百分点，本月汽车进口量价两端均明显收缩，而数量端收缩幅度更
大，反映国产车全面替代、进口车市场结构性退潮的长期趋势。粮食和大豆进
口数量同比降幅均有所扩大，本月农产品量价两端均为收缩，反映饲料需求偏
弱，粮食储备节奏偏慢。往后看，集成电路进口金额有望延续较高增长，但进
口数量增长偏弱，价格因素仍是重要驱动。资源品方面，原油价格走弱或削弱
进口金额增速，铜价仍可能提供支撑。整体来看，进口增长仍集中于 AI 相关
硬件，传统资源品及消费品进口需求尚未出现全面改善。
 风险提示：国内政策落地不及预期，海外经济波动超预期，地缘政治风险超预
期。

分析师承诺 
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明 
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准，新三板市场以三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。 
公司评级 
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上 
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-10%与 10%之间 
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-20%与-10%之间 
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-20%以下 
行业评级 
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%以下

### Page 1

M
Update
Broader recovery supports record earnings. 3Q revenue was NT$597.3bn (+1% Q/
Q, +82% Y/Y), with gross margin of 3.6% (+0.2ppt Q/Q, -0.1ppt Y/Y, 0.3ppt above
MSe), and net profit of NT$10.3bn (+6% Q/Q, +170% Y/Y, 42% above MSe). Data-
center revenue fell 5% Q/Q on shipment timing, while industrial, automotive and
Future Electronics revenue increased sequentially. The richer mix lifted gross margin
19bp Q/Q to 3.60%; operating margin rose 18bp to 2.38%, with operating expenses

increasing only 2% Q/Q. Net financing costs rose 22% Q/Q to NT$1.94bn.
AI drives a stronger 4Q. Management guides 4Q revenue of NT$730-770bn, with
the NT$750bn midpoint up 26% Q/Q, as data-center and communications growth
outweigh seasonal declines across consumer applications, industrial and automotive.
The guided gross-margin midpoint falls 36bp Q/Q to 3.24% on mix, but operating
profit rises 19% Q/Q to NT$16.96bn as expenses grow only 1%. 4Q EPS guidance is

NT$8.07–9.05, with the NT$8.56 midpoint up 7% Q/Q. Financing costs of NT$2.6–
2.8bn and the larger weighted-average share base temper the translation from
operating-profit growth into EPS.
Growth broadens, but cash conversion matters. Future Electronics grew over 40%
Y/Y in 3Q and has started shipping data-center products. Management remains
positive on 2027 AI demand, without providing a full-year earnings forecast.
Inventory days increased Q/Q from 47 to 66  and net debt from NT$27.6bn to NT

$46.4bn, although longer payables kept the cash-conversion cycle near 19 days
versus 18 in 2Q. We see further earnings potential from AI scale and Future’s
recovery, with funding requirements and inventory conversion the key execution
risks.
What’s Changed

WT expects a sharp 4Q revenue rebound as datacenter

shipments accelerate, supporting further profit growth despite a
less favorable mix. The key earnings sensitivity is how much
operating leverage offsets rising funding costs.

### Page 4

M
Update
4
Valuation methodology
We cut our price target by 15% to  NT$296: We update our residual income model to
reflect earnings estimate revisions. We increase our equity risk premium assumption from
11.8% to 12.5% to reflect segment volatilities, and reduce our intermediate growth rate
assumption from 10% to 8% to reflect a more conservative medium-term growth forecast.
We keep other key RI assumptions unchanged: 2.0% risk-free rate, 0.9 beta, cash dividend

payout ratio of 85%, and a long-term growth rate of 3.0%. Bull case PT is cut from NT
$406 to NT$344.5, implying 10.6x 2027 P/E. Bear case PT is cut from NT$230 to NT$160,
implying 4.9x 2027 P/E. The risk reward is now skewed more towards the bear case due to
potential downstream demand disruption.
Exhibit 3: WT: Residual Income Model
Source: Morgan Stanley Research estimates

Relative  valuation
Our price target implies 9.1x our 2027 EPS estimate, in line with its 8.9x average one-year
forward P/E since 2018, which we see as fair given improving demand and the company's
continued progress in penetrating the AI supply chain.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Document 117: 261109_daiwa_ Giant Manufacturing

Source PDF: all report pdf/261109_daiwa_ Giant Manufacturing.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: Giant announced its 3Q26 results after market hours on 9 
November 2026. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating on its improving 
fundamentals and the recovery of the global bike market. We believe 
inventory destocking is coming to an end.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results: missed on operating margin. Giant’s 
3Q26 revenue of TWD16.311bn (-2.1% QoQ, +6.2% YoY) was roughly in 
line with our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus. Its gross margin of 
23.9% beat our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus by 2.9pp and 
1.1pp, respectively, driven by product mix optimisation and new product 
launches in its own brand. However, its operating margin of 3.1% missed 
our estimate and the consensus by 1.4pp and 2.1pp, respectively, due to 
marketing activities for MY27 launches and legal fees related to the WRO. 
Thus, its 3Q26 operating profit of TWD510m (-49.0% QoQ, +28.9% YoY) 
missed our estimate and the consensus by 31.3% and 42.2%, respectively. 
Its 3Q26 net profit of TWD330m (-48.0% QoQ, -5.7% YoY) also missed our 
estimate and the consensus by 39.6% and 47.8%, respectively. Its 3Q26 
EPS finished at TWD0.84.

Demand recovery in Europe and China; a bright 2027. Giant sees 
gradual market recovery in Europe on new model launches and demand 
recovery in China. In the US, Giant continues to work towards having the 
WRO lifted, but will adjust production allocation to maximise the supply to 
meet demand in the US market. Its E-bike revenue accounted for 21% of 
9M26 revenue, which we believe reflects the declining OEM segment. 
Giant aims for its e-bike shipments to decline by 1% YoY, from 516,000 
units in 2025 to 510,000 in 2026, on weaker OE customer demand. For 
2026, it guides for sales to decline by low-single-digit % YoY, with the gross 
margin improving to 21-23% (from 20-21%) and an operating margin of 1-
3%. We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 5-30% after factoring in the 3Q26 
results with more conservative operating-margin estimates on marketing 
expenses.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call with an unchanged 
12M TP of TWD121, still based on our 1-year forward EPS forecast with an 
unchanged target PER of 22x to reflect its improved earnings trajectory, 
and we look for a better year in 2027. Key downside risks: slower-than-
expected e-bike penetration in the EU and macro/tariff uncertainties.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 8-26% below the consensus, likely 
as our revenue and operating margin forecasts are more conservative.

9 November 2026

3Q26 results review: missed on operating margin

➢ 3Q26 net profit missed our estimate and the consensus by 39-48% 
➢ Better-than-expected recovery in Europe and China; a brighter 2027 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating with an unchanged 12M TP of TWD121

Company profile 
Founded in 1972 and listed in 1994 on Taiwan’s main board, Giant is the largest bicycle maker in 
Taiwan, with an annual capacity of c.5m bicycles. In 2025, Asia accounted for 57% of its revenue, 
followed by Europe (33%) and America (7%). Around 74% of its 2025 revenue came from OBM (Giant, 
Liv, momentum) brands and 26% from OEM/ODM (Trek, Scott and Colnago) brands.

### Page 5

5

Giant Manufacturing (9921 TT): 9 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Aug 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson are not the same individual. Led by the Liu family, there are 11
members on the board. The founder and former Chairman is now a director, with his niece, Mrs.
Tu, leading Giant. We view Mrs. Tu's leadership to be strong, as she is well-recognised by the
former Chairman and the board for her capabilities, and she is one of the few female
chairpersons to lead a Taiwanese company.
Capital management 2 Giant has maintained its dividend payout ratio between 50% and 100% over the past decade , which we believe to be reasonable considering its global leadership status and expansion needs.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related-party transactions accounted for 58-62% of total revenue over 2019-25,
with sales mostly from its OBM (ASP varies with products/markets). We observed no suspicious
activity.
S Product quality & safety 1
According to Giant's 2023 ESG report, Giant as the global OEM/OBM leader in the bicycle
industry, product quality and safety are the top priority. We see ongoing R&D expenses and
relevant investment in new models and note that Giant has introduced its "Right Ride" system to
assist customers seeking suitable bike options.
S Supply chain management 1
Giant has developed its Giant production management system that diversifies its capacity
globally and utilises production resources fully to lower political or regional risk. As Giant has
control over its material sourcing, design process and manufacturing/OEM, we believe it has
solid supply chain management capabilities that allow for better cost control than peers.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Update
Lite-On Technology  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +6% MoM/+32%
YoY
In this report, we focus on Lite-On's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$20,071mn (+6% MoM/+32% YoY), driven by the
server PSU/BBU and opto semiconductors (couplers and indicators for
industrial automation, new energy and AI applications).
• QTD revenue reached 33% of both MSe at NT$60,395mn (+5% QoQ/+36%

YoY), and consensus at NT$60,353mn (+5% QoQ/+36% YoY).
• By segment, Cloud & AIoT/Optoelectronics/IT&CE accounted for
66%/13%/21% of the monthly sales, respectively, implying growth of +19%/-
1%/-17% MoM and  +71%/+7%/-16% YoY.
Our view:
• We think its 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We believe Lite-On will grow along with the AI capex cycle driven by its
power-related offerings, though execution and progress with new customers

remain key to watch.
• Stay EW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 15x based on average 2027-28 EPS estimates. We believe our target
multiple is justified by the 50% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of
0.3, vs. the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger optical business growth
n Stronger data center/server demand

n PC/NB demand growth
n Downtrend in raw material prices
Risks to Downside
n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

### Page 1

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC 으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500 억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O 에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7 억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

Document 327: First Impressions_ NZ retail card spending, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ retail card spending, October 2026 (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

#retail #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ retail card spending, October 2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:26 November 12 2026 Share
Card spending was surprisingly weak again in October.
Retail spending
Monthly change: -0.7% (prev: -0.5%, Westpac forecast: +1.4%)
Annual change: +0.3% (prev: +0.9%)

### Page 2

Core retail spending (excluding fuel/autos) 
Monthly change: -1.2% (prev: -0.7%) 
Annual change: -0.8% (prev: +0.1%)

Retail spending on credit and debit cards was reportedly down 0.7% in October, a third straight monthly
decline. Spending was down across all categories with the exception of fuel, which saw a 4.8% rise in
spending – and with fuel prices around 10% higher over the month, that still points to a decline in
spending volumes. 
We should note that we have had concerns for some time about the coverage of this data. The monthly
result was again significantly weaker than what we recorded for spending on Westpac-issued credit
and debit cards (ANZ also reported an increase in spending on its own figures). And as a lead indicator
of the quarterly retail survey (which feeds into GDP), it has undershot in every one of the last six
quarters. We don’t suggest that spending is growing strongly by any means – and it is likely the case
that higher fuel prices are crowding out spending elsewhere. But we suspect that the true picture is not
as weak as today’s release suggests. 
A drop in oil prices since the start of November has provided some relief for fuel prices at the pump,
although they remain high. We anticipate that growth in consumer spending volumes will remain
subdued in the near term, with regional variations dependent on exposure to the well-performing export
sector. What happens beyond this year will depend on how the Middle East conflict evolves and how
the RBNZ responds to the rebound in inflation.

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid-
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk, curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforces our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place: remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6-
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications: Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector: Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector. As we enter mid-cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress, S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3%, but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment-
grade corporates, offer attractive income. The 3-7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply: Since states 
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply-demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data-centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower-quality issuers. High-yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC-rated debt has reached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation, fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout, 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near-term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7. A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa. A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid-
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid-term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a defensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities, with clean energy stocks, including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters (see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook, 
the transition to mid-cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and AxJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the key takeaways from Q3 2026 US earnings? 
Our view: Q3 US earnings remain robust, but more modest 
earnings beats reinforce our view that equity markets are 
entering a mid-cycle phase. We remain Overweight US equities, 
but recommend investors to maintain diversified sector 
exposure as returns moderate and market leadership broadens. 
Rationale: As of 30 October, 82% of S&P500 companies that had 
reported had beaten earnings estimates. However, aggregate 
earnings were just 4.5% above expectations, a smaller beat than in 
recent quarters. Still, Q3 US earnings growth remains robust at 
31.6% y/y, supporting expected full-year growth of 36.1% in 2026 
and 16.4% in 2027 for the S&P500. The slower pace of upgrades 
suggests the bull market is maturing as investors focus on the 
sustainability of earnings growth. This raises the hurdle for positive 
share price reactions and makes selectivity more essential. For Big 
Tech, capex commitments remain high, while free cash flow is under 
pressure and expected to remain negative. Financing risks weigh on 
sentiment, particularly for companies with weaker balance sheets.  
We remain Overweight US equities, given the strong earnings 
growth, but we expect elevated volatility and modest returns. We 
prefer quality exposure within technology while maintaining 
diversified exposure across US financials, healthcare and materials. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  What to expect from Japanese banks’ upcoming results?   
Our view: We expect Japanese banks’ earnings growth to 
continue over the medium term, supported by higher domestic 
net interest income (NII) and resilient fee income. We remain 
positive on the sector, favouring banks with strong deposit 
franchises and scope to increase shareholder returns. 
Investors will focus on whether full-year guidance upgrades 
and buybacks exceed expectations. 
Rationale: The BoJ’s September rate hike to 1.25% took effect late 
in the quarter, so its benefit should be more visible from the 
December quarter as loans reprice. We expect three further BoJ 
rate hikes by June 2027, supporting further NII expansion. We 
will also look for signs that stronger corporate investment is driving 
faster loan growth and higher financing and advisory fees.  
We prefer to place more weight on guidance upgrades driven by 
recurring lending and fee income than on those driven by gains on 
equity disposals. Continued reductions in cross-shareholdings 
could free up capital for additional buybacks, providing a further 
boost to return on equity (ROE). Key risks: faster-than-expected 
deposit rate repricing, rising credit costs and bond portfolio losses. 
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the fundamental reasons behind your India 
equity upgrade? Which sectors offer favourable risk-reward? 
Our View: Indian equities offer an increasingly attractive risk-
reward profile. With India’s valuation premium to Emerging 
Markets (EMs) narrowing, foreign ownership near decade lows, 
Underweight EM positioning and a stabilising Indian rupee 
(INR), conditions are increasingly supportive of Indian equity 
outperformance vs. the broader AxJ region.  
Rationale: We have upgraded Indian equities to Overweight within 
our AxJ equities allocation. While Indian markets have witnessed 
USD 32bn of foreign investor outflows in 2026 year-to-date, strong 
domestic institutional investor inflows of USD 74bn have more 
than offset foreign selling. These outflows and the Indian equity 
market’s underperformance vs. global peers reflect broader EM 
allocation shifts towards AI, rather than weakening Indian 
fundamentals. Capital has gravitated towards markets such as the 
US, Taiwan and South Korea, which offer direct exposure to AI 
beneficiaries. However, we expect market leadership to broaden, as 
the rapid AI buildout is increasingly debt-funded, and US mid-term 
election results raise uncertainty. We prefer to diversify and we see 
investors reallocating towards structurally strong growth markets 
such as India, which offer non-AI driven growth.  
The RBI’s measures have supported INR stability, but underlying 
USD demand remains elevated. Lower oil prices would improve 
external balances, but the INR is likely to follow a path of gradual, 
controlled depreciation rather than a quick downward move. On 
valuation to its own historical average, the Nifty 50 one-year forward 
P/E is trading 18% below its long-term average while ROE at 14.7% 
is above historical averages. With India now marginally Underweight 
in large EM portfolios compared to the c.2% average Overweight 
historically, the case for a recovery in foreign flows is 
strengthening. 
India’s earnings growth remains intact. The Nifty 50 clocked Q1 
FY27 and Q2 FY27 profit-after-tax growth of c.20% y/y (ex-oil 
marketing companies). Large-caps offer greater earnings visibility 
and downside protection, while stronger growth in small- and mid-
caps warrants a selective approach given elevated valuations.  
India’s growth outlook remains supported by a strengthening 
investment cycle, manufacturing momentum, supply chain 
diversification, resilient consumption and healthy corporate and 
banking balance sheets. Importantly, earnings growth remains 
robust and broad-based across the financial, metals, telecom, 
automobiles and other domestic sectors, with Nifty 50 earnings 
growth expectations of c.14-15% over 2027 vis a vis AxJ’s 
technology driven earnings growth of 29% in 2027. 
—  Shaily Gang, Chief Investment Strategist, India

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the emerging US job market data alter your view of 
the Fed’s rate path and government bond yields? 
Our view: We do not expect the recent and incoming US job 
market data to alter our outlook unless there are significant 
deviations from the US economy’s current trajectory.  
Rationale: The US economy continues to exhibit resilience, with the 
latest Job Openings and Labour Turnover Survey data showing job 
openings surprising to the upside at 7.5mn. Initial jobless claims 
remain near three-month lows. There is little evidence of a material 
slowdown in labour demand. Inflation is expected to trend lower 
through H2 2027, but the disinflationary impulse remains a medium-
term story and is unlikely to manifest meaningfully in the near term. 
The market is now pricing in two additional 25bps hikes by H1 
2027 (down from three previously), in line with our view. These two 
rate hikes are largely priced into the US government bond yield 
curve. We estimate 2- and 10-year US government bond yields to 
range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, respectively, over the next 
three months. Our twelve-month forecast for 2- and 10-year US 
government bond yields are 4.25-4.5% and 4.75-5%, respectively, 
The 2-year yield is already trading within our expected range and 
would only move materially higher if a significantly stronger-than-
expected payrolls print led markets to price in a third hike. With two 
hikes baked in, long-run disinflation expectations intact and an 
uncertain fiscal outlook, the longer end of the yield curve is 
expected to be less sensitive to a single payrolls release. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your outlook for the GBP following the BoE’s rate 
hike this week? Do you see any impact on EUR/GBP? 
Our view: The BoE rate hike supports the GBP, but upside 
remains limited. We remain constructive on EUR/GBP, 
expecting a rebound towards 0.8450. 
Rationale: The BoE hiked by 25bps to 4% as expected. The GBP 
was little changed, despite hawkish inflation guidance. With markets 
pricing only around 50bps of additional BoE tightening over the next 
12 months, we see the GBP remaining vulnerable. In contrast, the 
ECB held its deposit rate at 2.50% in October but signalled readiness 
to tighten if inflation remains elevated. We expect Euro area inflation 
to rise further, keeping the ECB’s tightening bias alive. This suggests 
EUR/GBP has fallen too far relative to interest rate differentials.  
Germany’s planned increase in federal debt issuance should 
support Euro area growth, while Moody’s decision not to downgrade 
France has removed an important EUR tail risk. Meanwhile, tighter 
UK fiscal policy is likely to weigh on domestic growth and business 
investment. We expect EUR/GBP to recover towards 0.8450. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We expect 2- and 10-year US government bond 
yields to range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, 
respectively, over the next three months

### Page 8

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is the AUD underperforming despite the RBA hike? 
Our view: The AUD has lagged because markets had largely 
priced in the RBA’s November rate hike to 4.85%, and the bank 
gave investors less reason to expect another. Equity weakness 
also weighed on the currency. We remain bullish on AUD/USD 
and would add on pullbacks. Australia’s interest rate advantage 
and a sustained recovery in risk sentiment should support a 
move towards 0.72 in three months and 0.74 in 12 months. 
Rationale: Ahead of the 3 November RBA rate decision, market 
pricing implied an 88% chance of a 25bps hike, leaving little scope 
for an upside surprise. What changed after the announcement 
was the pricing of the next hike. The RBA dropped its reference 
to further rate increases, and three members preferred a rate hold. 
Markets scaled back the odds of rates reaching 5.10% next year to 
around 30%, thus weighing on the AUD. 
The AUD is sensitive to swings in global risk appetite, especially 
the US tech sell-off spread to global equities, triggering margin calls 
and weighing on the currency. An equity recovery would ease that 
pressure. Even after the two 25bps Fed hikes we expect by mid-
2027, Australia would retain a 35bps rate advantage over the top 
of the Fed’s target range, supporting a gradual AUD recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We see AUD/USD upside risks on a sustained 
recovery in risk sentiment

STOXX 600 Earnings Outlook provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                         November 5, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 25.0% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.5%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 11.9% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 6.9%.

 212 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 58.0% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 257 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 64.2% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of November 9, 40 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.2% above estimates, which is below the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 fo r Q3 2026 is 25%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €164.4B in Q3 2026, compared to shar e-weighted earnings of €131.6B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Eight of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improv ement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (153 .4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €35.7B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-69.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

The Consumer Cyclicals sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (-1.2%) of any sector. It is expected to generate 
sales of €196.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €199.1B in Q3 2025.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Share markets rallied across much of the globe. In the
US, reports of an upgraded outlook for CoreWeave, an
oversubscribed IPO for Anthropic and a stabilisation in
Nvidia supported renewed optimism around AI which

buoyed tech stocks, seeing the S&P 500 (+1.4%) and
Nasdaq (+1.7%) both rally, though the smaller gain for the
Dow Jones (+0.5%) shows the rally was more narrowly
based.
•  Across the pond, the Euro Stoxx 50 continued to build on
recent momentum with another solid gain (+1.0%), while
London’s FTSE 100 bounced back from a modest decline
yesterday (+0.8%). Stocks were up across most of Asia,
including Tokyo (+1.3%), Shanghai (+0.4%) and Seoul
(+2.6%), with only the Hang Seng (–1.0%) bucking the

wider trend. The ASX 200 rose 0.3% yesterday, but weaker
trading in futures overnight points to a mixed open this
morning.
•  Cash trading in bond markets were closed in the US for
Veterans Day, but trading in futures points to a continued
rise in yields following hotter-than-expected inflation
data received earlier in the week, which all but cemented
a December FOMC rate hike. Beyond December, markets
are also pricing in another two rate hikes over the course of

2027.
• Yields were little changed elsewhere, with the 10Y Bund
and Gilt steady on the day. The 10Y JGB meanwhile
continued to sell-off, with yields jumping 10bps to 2.80%
having effectively unwound the rally over late last week.
ACGBs were also weaker during local trading, seeing yields
rise 7bps on the 3Y and 6bps on the 10Y, following the
sharp rise in global yields the day prior post US CPI. Swaps
markets point to no hikes nor cuts from the RBA through to

end-2027 at present.
•  The USD received a solid bid overnight, buoyed by sharp
inflows into AI and tech stocks that improved risk sentiment.
The DXY finished 0.7% higher at around 102.9, the
strongest since April 2025. That move has pushed the Euro
down 0.6% back to a USD1.10 handle, the weakest since
May 2025, while the Yen slipped 0.7% to JPY159.9, the
weakest since the before the early-September rally. While
moves in the AUD was comparatively small overnight,

slipping 0.2% to around the USD0.6850 mark, that move
builds on recent downward momentum and has the currency
delicately positioned near 2026’s lows.
•  Oil prices rose slightly overnight, stabilising somewhat after
recent declines amid ongoing uncertainty around the future
of the US-Iran War, as President Trump downplays the
possibility of further strikes on Iran all while negotiations
show little progress. Brent rose 1.0% to US$92.4/bbl. The
rebound in risk sentiment buoyed metals, with LME copper

up 1.0% to US$13,817/mt while LME aluminium jumped
1.9% to US$3,068/mt. Gold prices meanwhile dipped 1.2%,
falling back below the US$4,000/oz mark, building on recent
momentum post US CPI which saw real yields jump higher
International Data
There was no major data out yesterday.
Domestic Data
In Australia, the value of dwelling finance approvals surprised
materially to the downside in Q3, the 15.5% decline marking
the largest quarterly decline on records going back to 1991.

The decline looks to be mostly volume-driven, with the number
of loans falling sharply compared to the average size of loans.
Across segments, investor lending values dropped by nearly a
third (see here).

Document 402: Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade

Source PDF: all report pdf/Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Global trade will remain resilient in 2027, but the focus will
shift from tariﬀs to ownership, technology and investment.
While not our base case, an escalation in an EU-China trade
war would rank among the most signiﬁcant risks to global
trade

Inga Fechner and Carsten Brzeski
Europe is undoubtedly facing a second 'China shock' – only this time around,
it's largely negative
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Trade survives politics
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

Global goods trade continues to weather geopolitical shocks.
Over the past few years, trade has proved far more resilient
than global headlines suggest. We expect that resilience to
continue into 2027. Disruptions in global shipping, shifting
tariﬀ regimes and recurring geopolitical tensions have
repeatedly altered trade routes without derailing trade itself.
Strong demand for AI-related investment and infrastructure
should continue to support trade ﬂows, particularly across

Asia, while selective US-China decoupling is likely to beneﬁt
third countries that attract production and investment. For
2027, we expect global goods trade to remain positive,
expanding about 3% year-on-year. In short, trade survives
politics in 2027 because companies can still adapt faster
than governments can restrict them.
Our risky call: Ownership matters more than
trade
Tariﬀs were only the ﬁrst step. By 2027, the real battleground shifts
to capital, technology and ownership. As companies invest behind

tariﬀ walls, governments stop merely screening new investment
and begin requiring companies to restructure, separate sensitive
operations or divest existing strategic assets.
A foreign-owned battery plant, semiconductor facility, or data
centre once supported for bringing local investment and jobs is
now treated as a national-security risk. Companies that once
navigated geopolitical tensions through diversiﬁcation ﬁnd
themselves forced to choose sides. The question is no longer who

exports the most, but who controls the productive capacity on
which economies depend. Trade would continue, but the corporate
structures that have kept it moving would start to fracture as
governments alter trade ﬂows. This could potentially cause global
goods trade growth to stall or even contract in 2027.
Our bold call: EU-China trade war

This publication has been prepared by ING solely for information purposes
irrespective of a particular user's means, ﬁnancial situation or investment

Read more
Download
Included in the following bundle
The EU and China enter an outright trade conﬂict. Europe abandons

its largely open-market approach and increasingly applies
reciprocity principles to market access and investment. Chinese
ﬁrms investing in strategic sectors face mandatory 50:50 joint
ventures, local ownership requirements and growing restrictions on
acquisitions.
At the same time, the EU imposes broad tariﬀs on Chinese imports,
with rates reaching 50% in politically sensitive sectors such as
vehicles, chemicals, machinery and electronics. China retaliates

with measures of similar scale.
The consequences are signiﬁcant. China accounts for roughly 15%
of global goods trade, while the EU accounts for around 13.5%.
Together they represent almost 30% of world goods trade. With the
EU being China's largest trading partner in goods and China being
the EU's second-largest trading partner in goods, a full-scale trade
conﬂict would rank among the most consequential economic
conﬂicts of the modern era.

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

agree the cyber threat landscape is
evolving faster than most organisations
can adapt

agree

agree the gap between highly and poorly
prepared organisations is widening

### Page 4

4
Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 10

Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?
dbDataInsights survey – September 2026, based on overseeing development while managing cyber risk

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015-2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

Marks & Spencer (Reported Apr 2025)
What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd
party forced the suspension of all online
orders and led to empty store shelves.
What did it cost?
~£136mn of direct costs related to the
attack, estimated group operating profit
impact of around £300mn for the FY. M&S
also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.
Coupang (Reported Nov 2025)
What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.
Equifax (Reported Sep 2017)
What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

Notable cyber attacks: how they happened, estimated costs and share price reaction, cont.
Nike (Reported Jan 2026)
What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product
specifications and pricing), following expiry
of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating
the incident, it did not report any
operational disruption. Underperformance

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.
Jaguar Land Rover (Reported Sep 2025)
What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.
